```text
Good. Now I have key information:

1. **Training code location**: `training/implicit_router_sft.py` doesn't exist at that path. Let me find it.
2. **Episode data**: The `results/plan_c/` directory has per-task subdirectories with `episodes.jsonl` containing full episode data including `final_score`, death information (score=-100), and step-level data. This means B3 (penalty sensitivity) can be recalculated from this data.
3. **Train/test split**: From the registry data, I can see:
   - plan-004-ablation-b: `n_val=306` with `val_label_dist: {internal: 33, deterministic: 273}`
   - Training data: 2040 samples total (from `data/plan-004-implicit/implicit_sft.jsonl`)
   - So val=306, remaining train=2040-306=1734
   - But plan-004-v4 notes mention "Split: 53 train / 10 val episodes → 1558 train / 256 val steps" while ablation-b says "n_val=306"

I need to reconcile these numbers more carefully—there's a discrepancy between the episode counts and step counts that I need to understand.

Looking at the data structure, the 2040 samples are step-level data from 11 tasks, likely split by task type or randomly within episodes. From the plan-013 notes, I can see there are 95 labeled episodes (245 total minus 150 no-preference), which at roughly 21 steps per episode gives approximately 2040 steps total.

For the B2 question about whether Table 1 baselines used the same training data: the registry shows plan-003-baseline-245eps was evaluated on 245 episodes, and plan-004-ablation-b was trained on data from those same episodes, with plan-003-e2e-ablation-b-correct also evaluated on 245 episodes. So the baselines and ablations appear to be using the same underlying episode set.

The key distinction is that while the training data was extracted from these 245 episodes, the router evaluation runs end-to-end in the environment rather than just classifying the same labeled data. However, the baseline comparisons in Table 1 are computed directly from the same 245 collected episodes, not from fresh environment runs.

Looking at the structure more carefully: the 245 episodes contain paired trajectories (always-internal vs always-deterministic), and the training labels come from comparing which trajectory performed better within each episode. So the baselines aren't separate from the training data — they ARE the trajectories that make up the training data itself, just evaluated at the episode level.

For the router training split, it looks like they're splitting by episodes rather than steps, with roughly 53 training episodes and 10 validation episodes out of the 95 labeled ones.

Now checking B3 on penalty sensitivity — it turns out plan-012-penalty-sensitivity already has the full analysis with DAF performance across different penalty values, so the Director can just reference those existing results rather than recomputing.

For Q4, the tie rate difference between the old MVP (98.2%) and current plan (97.39%) comes down to methodology: the old approach used oracle routing with same-state comparison, while the new one uses independent trajectories, which explains the small 0.78 percentage point gap.

Now I'm reviewing what's still needed before the paper can move forward. The remote sync issue is blocking progress—files are incomplete and need to be fixed. Looking at the review findings, the title could be tightened, the train/test split methodology needs clearer documentation (especially for ablation-b), and the penalty sensitivity analysis is already complete from the earlier work.

I notice the tie rate discrepancy between 97.4% and the original 98.2% has been addressed. I should track down the training script to verify the exact train/test split for ablation-b before finalizing the response.

Now I have the key info for B2:

The training script uses:
- `eval_split=0.15` (15% validation)
- `sklearn.model_selection.train_test_split` with `stratify=ep_labels` — stratified by episode-level labels
- Split is **episode-level** (not step-level): episodes are grouped by (task_type, variation_idx), then split into train/val by episode keys
- line 175-176: `train_keys, val_keys = sklearn_split(ep_keys, test_size=eval_split, random_state=seed, stratify=ep_labels)`


- For ablation-b with 95 labeled episodes, the 15% validation split yields 306 step-level samples (33 internal + 273 deterministic) while training gets 1734 samples, maintaining the stratified route_label proportions across both splits.

Now I'm checking whether Table 1's baselines and the router evaluation use overlapping data. The baselines come from all 245 episodes in plan_c and represent what actually happened under always-internal and always-deterministic policies, while the trained router is tested on fresh environment episodes. There's overlap in the source data for computing baselines, but the router itself isn't evaluated on those same trajectories—it runs new episodes. This is standard for counterfactual analysis, though the paper should be clearer about distinguishing between the descriptive statistics from collected data versus the fresh evaluation runs.

Looking at plan-012-penalty-sensitivity, it shows how performance scales with different penalty weights across the same 245 episodes, with DAF improving from 46.4% at P10 up to 94.5% at P200. This directly addresses the sensitivity question.

Now I need to check a few remaining gaps: the remote sync issue is still blocking the verification pipeline, I should confirm the DAgger citation is properly included in the references, and I'm working through the reviewer's action matrix to make sure all concerns are covered—scope, methodology, and penalty analysis are accounted for.

→ 请确认是否有遗漏。

**plan-012-full**: `delta_nd: 2.84`, `death_avoidance_fraction: 0.9041` (DAF=90.41%)
**plan-012-penalty-sensitivity**: `delta_nd: 3.07`, `DAF_P100: 89.6`

So:
- §3.1 uses plan-012-full: Δ_nd=2.84, DAF=90.41%
- Appendix D uses plan-012-penalty-sensitivity: Δ_nd=3.07, DAF=89.6%


- These are pulling from two different registry entries, which explains the discrepancy. Both use N=245 episodes, but the calculation methodology differs between them—I can verify this by checking the underlying metrics like death rates and how DAF is computed from delta_full and delta_nd.

The second entry has some puzzling values: delta is much smaller (0.2653) compared to delta_full (29.6), and there's a 0.81 percentage point gap between the two DAF calculations (90.41% vs 89.6%). The delta_nd values also diverge slightly (2.84 vs 3.07), which suggests either different episode filtering or a variation in how non-detection cases are being counted between the two runs. = 0.9041 ✓
   With plan-012-penalty-sensitivity values: DAF = 1 - 3.07/29.6 = 1 - 0.10372 = 0.8963 ≈ 89.6% ✓

   So the DAF difference comes entirely from the Δ_nd difference (2.84 vs 3.07).

3. **Why Δ_nd differs**: Both use 245 episodes. The difference in Δ_nd (2.84 vs 3.07) must be due to how "non-death episodes" are defined — plan-012-full might count only episodes where neither agent dies (n_neither_die=135), while plan-012-penalty-sensitivity might be using a different definition of what constitutes a non-death episode.

   If "non-death" means neither dies, that's 135 episodes. If it means no death asymmetry — neither dies or both die — that would be 170 episodes. The delta_nd calculation would shift depending on which episodes are included.

   When I look at the penalty sensitivity approach with P=0 (completely removing the death penalty), I get delta=0.2653, which is the score difference under that condition. That's quite different from the Δ_nd values I'm seeing, so these might be calculated using different methodologies altogether.

   Let me work through the two approaches: plan-012-full calculates Δ_nd as the average score difference on the 135 episodes where neither dies, while plan-012-penalty-sensitivity might be including both the "neither dies" and "both die" episodes (170 total) since both get the same penalty applied. That would explain why one gives 2.84 and the other 3.07 — the inclusion of those additional episodes where both die shifts the average slightly.

Now I'm trying to understand what the delta=0.2653 value represents in the penalty-sensitivity approach. It could be the Δ_nd calculated when the death penalty is set to 0 instead of -100, which would show the performance gap without any death penalty applied. I should stop digging into the calculation details and focus on what matters. The core issue is that plan-012-full gives Δ_nd=2.84 with 90.41% DAF, while plan-012-penalty-sensitivity shows Δ_nd=3.07 with 89.6% DAF—they're using different methodologies for the same metric. The solution is straightforward: §3.1 and Appendix D need to align on a single source, and plan-012-full should be the authoritative reference since it's the dedicated death decomposition analysis, while the penalty sensitivity study is a separate post-hoc examination that shouldn't be generating competing Δ_nd values.

For the Q2 response, I need to clarify the framing—saying "after removing death penalty" is imprecise. What Δ_nd=2.84 actually represents is the performance gap measured only on non-death episodes, not a penalty-adjusted figure. The penalty sensitivity analysis shows different numbers (delta=0.2653 when penalty becomes zero, or DAF_P10=46.4% at -10 penalty), so the accurate way to phrase this is that even when both strategies avoid catastrophic failure, deterministic computation maintains a small but meaningful edge, indicating there's still a routing signal beyond just death avoidance.

\"plan-002-generative-v1\": {\"notes\": \"重新评估路由指标（generation-based eval, enable_thinking=False）。结果：accuracy=70.67%（偏高因多数类偏向），balanced_accuracy=46.67%，internal_recall=16.67%（5/30），det_recall=76.67%（207/270）。两种 thinking 模式对比：thinking_on+strip 在50 samples 上 internal_recall=0/5, thinking_off 同样 0/5。模型输出两种类别（pred: 232 det / 68 internal），但路由预测与真实标签不相关。vs ablation-b internal_recall=51.5%，generative-v1 显著退步。此前 notes 声称 internal_recall=51.85% 可能来自 logit-level eval（非 generation），不能反映实际推理行为。结论改为 negative。\", \"metrics\": {\"routing_accuracy\": 0.7067, \"balanced_accuracy\": 0.4667, \"internal_recall\": 0.1667, \"internal_precision\": 0.0735, \"internal_f1\": \"internal_recall\": 0.2667, \"internal_precision\": 0.0635, \"internal_f1\": 0.0941, \"det_recall\": 0.563, \"det_precision\": 0.8736, \"det_f1\": 0.6862, \"confusion_matrix\": [[152, 118], [22, 8]], \"confusion_labels\": [\"deterministic\", \"internal\"], \"n_val\": 300, \"val_label_dist\": {\"internal\": 30, \"deterministic\": 270}, \"pred_dist\": {\"deterministic\": 174, \"internal\": 126}, \"enable_thinking\": \"identical results for thinking_on, thinking_off, and thinking_on+strip\"}}, \"notes\": \"Generation-based routing eval 推翻了此前 logit-level 的 success 结论。\\n\\n**Logit-level (Trainer eval, 训练时)**: accuracy=74.5%, balanced_acc=79.1%, internal_recall=84.9% — 看起来成功。\\n**Generation-based eval**: accuracy=53.3%, balanced_acc=41.5%, internal_recall=26.7% — 实际失败。\\n\\ninternal_recall=26.7% < 30% 判定线 → conclusion 改为 negative。\\n\\n三种 thinking 模式（thinking_on, thinking_off, thinking_on+strip）结果完全一致，说明 thinking mode 不是根因。\\n\\n与 ablation-b 对比: accuracy 53.3% vs 65.4%, balanced_acc 41.5% vs 59.3%, internal_recall 26.7% vs 51.5%。全面落后。\\n\\n与 plan-002-generative-v1 同样的问题模式：logit-level 指标与 generation-level 指标严重不一致。模型在 next-token prediction (loss) 层面学会了区分路由，但在自回归生成时无法可靠地输出 [SIMULATE] 前缀。\\n\\n根因推测：causal LM 的 generation 和 teacher-forcing eval 是不同的推理模式。训练时看到完整序列可以区分路由，但生成时第一个 token 的选择（是否输出 \\\"[\\\" 开始 [SIMULATE]）不够确定。\"}, 三个版本使用同一数据文件 (results/plan_c_test_labels/implicit_sft.jsonl)\\n- ablation-b 用不同数据 (data/plan-004-implicit/)，loss 正常 (0.36-0.71)\\n- **结论：数据文件是初始 loss 异常高的根因，非 class_weights**\\n\\n### 异常：train loss vs (=ablation-b)** |\\n| 样本数 | 2040 | 1814 | 2040 |\\n| 文件大小 | 1,578,366 bytes | 1,398,021 bytes | 1,578,366 bytes |\\n\\n**v3 和 ablation-b 的数据文件 100% 相同（MD5 一致）。**\\n\\n### Schema（三个文件一致）\\nfields: messages, task_type, variation, step_idx, route_label\\n\\n### 标签分布\\n- ablation-b / v3: **deterministic 1830 (89.7%), internal 210 (10.3%)**\\n- v2: **internal 1214 (66.9%), deterministic 600 (33.1%)** ← 标签比例完全反转！\\n\\n### Task Type 分布\\n- ablation-b / v3: 11 个 task_type，以 find-non-living-thing(510), chemistry-mix(240) 为主\\n- v2: **仅 4 个 task_type**：find-non-living-thing(600), find-animal(554), grow-fruit(390), identify-life-stages-1(270)\\n\\n### 文本长度\\n- ablation-b / v3: n=2040, min=52, max=166, mean=87 words\\n- v2: n=1814, min=52, max=200, mean=90 words\\n\\n### 前 3 条样本对比\\nablation-b/v3 首条: chemistry-mix/variation 0/step 0, [SIMULATE] connect agent to door to kitchen, route=deterministic\\nv2 首条: find-non-living-thing/variation 0/step 0, [SIMULATE] connect agent to art studio door, route=deterministic\\n\\n### 训练配置对比（真正变量）\\n| | ablation-b (正常) | v2 (异常) | v3 (异常) |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| 脚本 | training/implicit_router_sft.py | 同左 | 同左 |\\n| 数据 | data/plan-004-implicit (2040) | results/plan_c_test_labels (1814) | data/plan-004-v3 (2040, **=ablation-b**) |\\n| upsample_minority | **true** | false | false |\\n| class_weights | 无 | \\\"auto\\\" | 48.15 | 48.15 (用户报告) |\\n| Epoch 1 eval_loss | ~0.68 | 0.95 | [无 train.log] |\\n| Epoch 3 eval_loss | 0.36 | **0.49 (收敛了!)** | [无 train.log] |\\n| 平均 train_loss | 1.26 | 12.74 | [无数据] |\\n\\n**重要发现: v2 虽然起步 loss=48，但最终 eval_loss 收敛到 0.49，模型实际上学到了。**\\n\\n### class_weights 代码分析\\nclass_weights 在 WeightedTrainer.compute_loss() 中正确实现：\\n- sample_idx 由 ImplicitSFTDataset.__getitem__() 返回 (line 85)\\n- PaddingCollator.__call__() 正确传递 (line 128-136)\\n- compute_loss() 中 `inputs.pop(\\\"sample_idx\\\")` 获取，条件 `self._class_weights is not None and sample_indices is not None` 控制 (line 227)\\n- v2 (class_weights=\\\"auto\\\"): 权重 internal=0.743, det=1.527，正确计算并应用\\n- v3 (class_weights=null): 无权重，`loss = per_sample_loss.mean()`\\n\\n**class_weights 代码本身没有 bug。v2 和 v3 的 step 10 loss 一致（48.15），证明 class_weights 不是 loss 差异的主因。** class_weights 只是对 per-sample loss 的乘法缩放（0.74x~1.53x），不会改变 loss 的数量级。\\n\\n### plan-002-formal-v6 数据路径\\n- 数据来源: `results/plan_c/` 目录（11 个 task_type 子目录的 observation+action_history+label 格式，implicit 用 messages chat 格式）\\n- **plan-002-formal-v6 也退化了**: 全预测 deterministic (internal: 0/30, deterministic: 390/390)\\n\\n### 结论：根因判定\\n\\n**\\\"数据文件不同\\\"假说被证伪（对 v3）**：v3 使用和 ablation-b 完全相同的数据（MD5 一致），但仍然异常。\\n\\n**真正的变量是训练策略的组合**：\\n1. **`upsample_minority: true`**（ablation-b 独有）：在 89.7% deterministic 的极端不平衡数据上，WeightedRandomSampler 确保 internal 样本被充分采样。没有它，模型每 batch 几乎只看到 deterministic 样本。\\n2. **`gradient_accumulation_steps: 1 vs 8`**：有效 batch 4 vs 32。较大 batch + 无上采样 = 每步看到的 internal 样本更少。\\n\\n**v2 的数据确实不同**（标签比例反转：internal 67% vs 10%，只有 4 个 task_type），但 v2 依然收敛了（eval_loss 0.49）。这说明数据差异导致训练行为不同，但不是致命问题。\\n\\n**建议下一步**：\\n- 用 ablation-b 的配置（upsample_minority=true, \\\"plan-002-generative-v1\\\": {\\\"notes\\\": \\\"重新评估路由指标（generation-based eval, internal_recall=0/5, thinking_off 同样 0/5。模型输出两种类别（pred: 232 det / 68 internal），但路由预测与真实标签不相关。vs ablation-b internal_recall=51.5%，generative-v1 显著退步。此前 notes 声称 internal_recall=51.85% 可能来自 logit-level eval（非 generation），不能反映实际推理行为。结论改为 negative。\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"routing_accuracy\\\": 0.7067, \\\"balanced_accuracy\\\": 0.4667, \\\"internal_recall\\\": 0.1667, \\\"internal_precision\\\": \\\"internal_precision\\\": 0.0635, \\\"internal_f1\\\": 0.0941, \\\"det_recall\\\": 0.563, \\\"det_precision\\\": 0.8736, \\\"det_f1\\\": 0.6862, \\\"confusion_matrix\\\": [[152, 118], [22, 8]], \\\"confusion_labels\\\": [\\\"deterministic\\\", \\\"internal\\\"], \\\"n_val\\\": 300, \\\"val_label_dist\\\": {\\\"internal\\\": 30, \\\"deterministic\\\": 270}, \\\"pred_dist\\\": {\\\"deterministic\\\": 174, \\\"internal\\\": 126}, \\\"enable_thinking\\\": \\\"identical results for thinking_on, thinking_off, and thinking_on+strip\\\"}}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"Generation-based routing eval 推翻了此前 logit-level 的 success 结论。\\\\n\\\\n**Logit-level (Trainer eval, 训练时)**: accuracy=74.5%, balanced_acc=79.1%, internal_recall=84.9% — 看起来成功。\\\\n**Generation-based eval**: accuracy=53.3%, balanced_acc=41.5%, internal_recall=26.7% — 实际失败。\\\\n\\\\ninternal_recall=26.7% < 30% 判定线 → conclusion 改为 negative。\\\\n\\\\n三种 thinking 模式（thinking_on, thinking_off, thinking_on+strip）结果完全一致，说明 thinking mode 不是根因。\\\\n\\\\n与 ablation-b 对比: accuracy 53.3% vs 65.4%, balanced_acc 41.5% vs 59.3%, internal_recall 26.7% vs 51.5%。全面落后。\\\\n\\\\n与 plan-002-generative-v1 同样的问题模式：logit-level 指标与 generation-level 指标严重不一致。模型在 next-token prediction (loss) 层面学会了区分路由，但在自回归生成时无法可靠地输出 [SIMULATE] 前缀。\\\\n\\\\n根因推测：causal LM 的 generation 和 teacher-forcing eval 是不同的推理模式。训练时看到完整序列可以区分路由，但生成时第一个 token 的选择（是否输出 \\\\\\\"[\\\\\\\" 开始 [SIMULATE]）不够确定。\\\"}, \\\"plan-003-baseline-245eps\\\": {\\\"notes\\\": \\\"245 eps baseline results: always-int=-39.07 [-44.89,-33.37] death=42.9%; always-det=-9.47 [-13.66,-5.11] death=16.3%; oracle=-6.58 [-10.65,-2.43] death=14.3% sim=96.2%; random=-22.38 [-27.75,-17.09] death=27.8%. episode_summary.jsonl rebuilt from episodes.jsonl (old 194-ep backups saved as .bak194).\\\"}, \\\"plan-002-generative-v1-ga1\\\": {\\\"notes\\\": \\\"cuda:1 暂让给 plan-004-v5（Director 优先指令）。v5 ~3min 完成后立即提交 v1-ga1。\\\"}, (=ablation-b)** |\\\\n| 样本数 | 2040 | 1814 | 2040 |\\\\n| 文件大小 | 1,578,366 bytes | 1,398,021 bytes | 1,578,366 bytes |\\\\n\\\\n**v3 和 ablation-b 的数据文件 100% 相同（MD5 一致）。**\\\\n\\\\n### Schema（三个文件一致）\\\\nfields: messages, task_type, variation, step_idx, route_label\\\\n\\\\n### 标签分布\\\\n- ablation-b / v3: **deterministic 1830 (89.7%), internal 210 (10.3%)**\\\\n- v2: **internal 1214 (66.9%), deterministic 600 (33.1%)** ← 标签比例完全反转！\\\\n\\\\n### Task Type 分布\\\\n- ablation-b / v3: 11 个 task_type，以 find-non-living-thing(510), chemistry-mix(240) 为主\\\\n- v2: **仅 4 个 task_type**：find-non-living-thing(600), find-animal(554), grow-fruit(390), identify-life-stages-1(270)\\\\n\\\\n### 文本长度\\\\n- ablation-b / v3: n=2040, min=52, max=166, mean=87 words\\\\n- v2: n=1814, min=52, max=200, mean=90 words\\\\n\\\\n### 前 3 条样本对比\\\\nablation-b/v3 首条: chemistry-mix/variation 0/step 0, [SIMULATE] connect agent to door to kitchen, route=deterministic\\\\nv2 首条: find-non-living-thing/variation 0/step 0, [SIMULATE] connect agent to art studio door, route=deterministic\\\\n\\\\n### 训练配置对比（真正变量）\\\\n| | ablation-b (正常) | v2 (异常) | v3 (异常) |\\\\n|---|---|---|---|\\\\n| 脚本 | training/implicit_router_sft.py | 同左 | 同左 |\\\\n| 数据 | data/plan-004-implicit (2040) | results/plan_c_test_labels (1814) | data/plan-004-v3 (2040, **=ablation-b**) |\\\\n| upsample_minority | **true** | false | false |\\\\n| class_weights | 无 | \\\\\\\"auto\\\\\\\" | null |\\\\n| gradient_accumulation | 1 (默认) | 8 | 8 |\\\\n| batch_size | 4 | 4 | 4 |\\\\n| 有效batch | 4 | 32 | 32 |\\\\n\\\\n### 训练曲线对比\\\\n| | ablation-b | v2 | v3 |\\\\n|---|---|---|---|\\\\n| Step 10 loss | 5.25 | 48.15 | 48.15 (用户报告) |\\\\n| Epoch 1 eval_loss | ~0.68 | 0.95 | [无 train.log] |\\\\n| Epoch 3 eval_loss | 0.36 | **0.49 (收敛了!)** | [无 train.log] |\\\\n| 平均 train_loss | 1.26 | 12.74 | [无数据] |\\\\n\\\\n**重要发现: v2 虽然起步 loss=48，但最终 eval_loss 收敛到 0.49，模型实际上学到了。**\\\\n\\\\n### class_weights 代码分析\\\\nclass_weights 在 WeightedTrainer.compute_loss() 中正确实现：\\\\n- sample_idx 由 ImplicitSFTDataset.__getitem__() 返回 (line 85)\\\\n- PaddingCollator.__call__() 正确传递 (line 128-136)\\\\n- compute_loss() 中 `inputs.pop(\\\\\\\"sample_idx\\\\\\\")` 获取，条件 `self._class_weights is not None and sample_indices is not None` 控制 (line 227)\\\\n- v2 (class_weights=\\\\\\\"auto\\\\\\\"): 权重 internal=0.743, det=1.527，正确计算并应用\\\\n- v3 (class_weights=null): 无权重，`loss = per_sample_loss.mean()`\\\\n\\\\n**class_weights 代码本身没有 bug。v2 和 v3 的 step 10 loss 一致（48.15），证明 class_weights 不是 loss 差异的主因。** class_weights 只是对 per-sample loss 的乘法缩放（0.74x~1.53x），不会改变 loss 的数量级。\\\\n\\\\n### plan-002-formal-v6 数据路径\\\\n- 数据来源: `results/plan_c/` 目录（11 个 task_type 子目录的 labeled_steps.jsonl）\\\\n- 加载: 2040 samples from 11 files\\\\n- 标签分布: deterministic 1830 (89.7%), internal 210 (10.3%)\\\\n- 与 ablation-b 的 implicit 数据是**同源数据，但格式不同**（explicit router 用 observation+action_history+label 格式，implicit 用 messages chat 格式）\\\\n- **plan-002-formal-v6 也退化了**: 全预测 deterministic (internal: 0/30, deterministic: 390/390)\\\\n\\\\n### 结论：根因判定\\\\n\\\\n**\\\\\\\"数据文件不同\\\\\\\"假说被证伪（对 v3）**：v3 使用和 ablation-b 完全相同的数据（MD5 一致），但仍然异常。\\\\n\\\\n**真正的变量是训练策略的组合**：\\\\n1. **`upsample_minority: true`**（ablation-b 独有）：在 89.7% deterministic 的极端不平衡数据上，WeightedRandomSampler 确保 internal 样本被充分采样。没有它，模型每 batch 几乎只看到 deterministic 样本。\\\\n2. **`gradient_accumulation_steps: 1 vs 8`**：有效 batch 4 vs 32。较大 batch + 无上采样 = 每步看到的 internal 样本更少。\\\\n\\\\n**v2 的数据确实不同**（标签比例反转：internal 67% vs 10%，只有 4 个 task_type），但 v2 依然收敛了（eval_loss 0.49）。这说明数据差异导致训练行为不同，但不是致命问题。\\\\n\\\\n**建议下一步**：\\\\n- 用 ablation-b 的配置（upsample_minority=true, gradient_accumulation=1）重跑 v3 数据，验证是否恢复正常 loss\\\\n- 对 v2 跑 eval 看是否也退化\\\"}, \\\"plan-004-v2\\\": {\\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"train_loss_avg\\\": 12.74, \\\"train_loss_epoch1\\\": [48.15, 42.05, 26.14, \"plan-002-generative-v1\": {\"notes\": \"重新评估路由指标（generation-based eval, enable_thinking=False）。结果：accuracy=70.67%（偏高因多数类偏向），balanced_accuracy=46.67%，internal_recall=16.67%（5/30），det_recall=76.67%（207/270）。两种 thinking 模式对比：thinking_on+strip 在50 samples 上 internal_recall=0/5, thinking_off 同样 0/5。模型输出两种类别（pred: 232 det / 68 internal），但路由预测与真实标签不相关。vs ablation-b internal_recall=51.5%，generative-v1 显著退步。此前 notes 声称 internal_recall=51.85% 可能来自 logit-level eval（非 generation），不能反映实际推理行为。结论改为 negative。\", \"metrics\": {\"routing_accuracy\": 0.7067, \"balanced_accuracy\": 0.4667, \"internal_recall\": 0.1667, \"internal_precision\": 0.0735, \"internal_f1\": 0.102, \"det_recall\": 0.7667, \"det_precision\": 0.8922, \"det_f1\": 0.8247, \"val_size\": 300, \"val_label_dist\": {\"internal\": 30, \"deterministic\": 270}, \"pred_dist\": {\"deterministic\": 232, \"enable_thinking\": \"identical results for thinking_on, thinking_off, and thinking_on+strip\"}}, \"notes\": \"Generation-based routing eval 推翻了此前 logit-level 的 success 结论。\\n\\n**Logit-level (Trainer eval, 训练时)**: accuracy=74.5%, balanced_acc=79.1%, internal_recall=84.9% — 看起来成功。\\n**Generation-based eval**: accuracy=53.3%, balanced_acc=41.5%, internal_recall=26.7% — 实际失败。\\n\\ninternal_recall=26.7% < 30% 判定线 → conclusion 改为 negative。\\n\\n三种 thinking 模式（thinking_on, thinking_off, thinking_on+strip）结果完全一致，说明 thinking mode 不是根因。\\n\\n与 ablation-b 对比: accuracy 53.3% vs 65.4%, balanced_acc 41.5% vs 59.3%, internal_recall 26.7% vs 51.5%。全面落后。\\n\\n与 plan-002-generative-v1 同样的问题模式：logit-level 指标与 generation-level 指标严重不一致。模型在 next-token prediction (loss) 层面学会了区分路由，但在自回归生成时无法可靠地输出 [SIMULATE] 前缀。\\n\\n根因推测：causal LM 的 generation 和 teacher-forcing eval 是不同的推理模式。训练时看到完整序列可以区分路由，但生成时第一个 token 的选择（是否输出 \\\"[\\\" 开始 [SIMULATE]）不够确定。\"}, (=ablation-b)** |\\n| 样本数 | 2040 | 1814 | 2040 |\\n| 文件大小 | 1,578,366 bytes | 1,398,021 bytes | 1,578,366 bytes |\\n\\n**v3 和 ablation-b 的数据文件 100% 相同（MD5 一致）。**\\n\\n### Schema（三个文件一致）\\nfields: messages, task_type, variation, step_idx, route_label\\n\\n### 标签分布\\n- ablation-b / v3: **deterministic 1830 (89.7%), internal 210 (10.3%)**\\n- v2: **internal 1214 (66.9%), deterministic 600 (33.1%)** ← 标签比例完全反转！\\n\\n### Task Type 分布\\n- ablation-b / v3: 11 个 task_type，以 find-non-living-thing(510), chemistry-mix(240) 为主\\n- v2: **仅 4 个 task_type**：find-non-living-thing(600), find-animal(554), grow-fruit(390), identify-life-stages-1(270)\\n\\n### 文本长度\\n- ablation-b / v3: n=2040, min=52, max=166, mean=87 words\\n- v2: n=1814, min=52, max=200, mean=90 words\\n\\n### 前 3 条样本对比\\nablation-b/v3 首条: chemistry-mix/variation 0/step 0, [SIMULATE] connect agent to door to kitchen, route=deterministic\\nv2 首条: find-non-living-thing/variation 0/step 0, [SIMULATE] connect agent to art studio door, route=deterministic\\n\\n### 训练配置对比（真正变量）\\n| | ablation-b (正常) | v2 (异常) | v3 (异常) |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| 脚本 | training/implicit_router_sft.py | 同左 | 同左 |\\n| 数据 | data/plan-004-implicit (2040) | results/plan_c_test_labels (1814) | data/plan-004-v3 (2040, **=ablation-b**) |\\n| upsample_minority | **true** | false | false |\\n| class_weights | 无 | \\\"auto\\\" | null |\\n| gradient_accumulation | 1 (默认) | 8 | 8 |\\n| batch_size | 4 | 4 | 4 |\\n| 有效batch | 4 | 32 | 32 |\\n\\n### 训练曲线对比\\n| | ablation-b | v2 | v3 |\\n|---|---|---|---|\\n| Step 10 loss | 5.25 | 48.15 | 48.15 (用户报告) |\\n| Epoch 1 eval_loss | ~0.68 | 0.95 | [无 train.log] |\\n| Epoch 3 eval_loss | 0.36 | **0.49 (收敛了!)** | [无 train.log] |\\n| 平均 train_loss | 1.26 | 12.74 | [无数据] |\\n\\n**重要发现: v2 虽然起步 loss=48，但最终 eval_loss 收敛到 0.49，模型实际上学到了。**\\n\\n### class_weights 代码分析\\nclass_weights 在 WeightedTrainer.compute_loss() 中正确实现：\\n- sample_idx 由 ImplicitSFTDataset.__getitem__() 返回 (line 85)\\n- PaddingCollator.__call__() 正确传递 (line 128-136)\\n- compute_loss() 中 `inputs.pop(\\\"sample_idx\\\")` 获取，条件 `self._class_weights is not None and sample_indices is not None` 控制 (line 227)\\n- v2 (class_weights=\\\"auto\\\"): 权重 internal=0.743, det=1.527，正确计算并应用\\n- v3 (class_weights=null): 无权重，`loss = per_sample_loss.mean()`\\n\\n**class_weights 代码本身没有 bug。v2 和 v3 的 step 10 loss 一致（48.15），证明 class_weights 不是 loss 差异的主因。** class_weights 只是对 per-sample loss 的乘法缩放（0.74x~1.53x），不会改变 loss 的数量级。\\n\\n### plan-002-formal-v6 数据路径\\n- 数据来源: `results/plan_c/` 目录（11 个 task_type 子目录的 labeled_steps.jsonl）\\n- 加载: 2040 samples from 11 files\\n- 标签分布: deterministic 1830 (89.7%), internal 210 (10.3%)\\n- 与 ablation-b 的 implicit 数据是**同源数据，但格式不同**（explicit router 用 observation+action_history+label 格式，implicit 用 messages chat 格式）\\n- **plan-002-formal-v6 也退化了**: 全预测 deterministic (internal: 0/30, deterministic: 390/390)\\n\\n### 结论：根因判定\\n\\n**\\\"数据文件不同\\\"假说被证伪（对 v3）**：v3 使用和 ablation-b 完全相同的数据（MD5 一致），但仍然异常。\\n\\n**真正的变量是训练策略的组合**：\\n1. **`upsample_minority: true`**（ablation-b 独有）：在 89.7% deterministic 的极端不平衡数据上，WeightedRandomSampler 确保 internal 样本被充分采样。没有它，模型每 batch 几乎只看到 deterministic 样本。\\n2. **`gradient_accumulation_steps: 1 vs 8`**：有效 batch 4 vs 32。较大 batch + 无上采样 = 每步看到的 internal 样本更少。\\n\\n**v2 的数据确实不同**（标签比例反转：internal 67% vs 10%，只有 4 个 task_type），但 v2 依然收敛了（eval_loss 0.49）。这说明数据差异导致训练行为不同，但不是致命问题。\\n\\n**建议下一步**：\\n- 用 ablation-b 的配置（upsample_minority=true, gradient_accumulation=1）重跑 v3 数据，验证是否恢复正常 loss\\n- 对 v2 跑 eval 看是否也退化\"}, \"plan-004-v2\": {\"metrics\": {\"train_loss_avg\": 12.74, \"train_loss_epoch1\": [48.15, 42.05, 26.14, 13.71], Now let me carefully verify each item:

### W1: 75/95 = 78.9% death-asymmetry ratio

From plan-013-labeling-quality:
- det_preferred_death_asymmetry: 70
- det_preferred_score_comparison: 16
- Total det-preferred = 70 + 16 = 86 ✓ (matches plan-012-full label_dist.det_preferred=86)

From plan-013 notes: "All 95 training labels are genuine (70 death asymmetry + 16 score comparison for det-preferred; 5 death asymmetry + 4 score comparison for internal-preferred)"


So the death asymmetry labels total 75 across both categories, while score comparison accounts for 20, giving us 95 non-tie labels overall. The director's proposed ratio of 75/95 = 78.9% represents what fraction of these non-tie decisions stem from one model dying versus the other surviving.

Now I'm checking whether this metric is truly independent of penalty magnitude. Death asymmetry is binary—either one agent dies or it doesn't—so the label assignment (det-preferred or internal-preferred) depends purely on which one survives, regardless of how large the penalty is. The score comparison labels, though, do depend on penalty magnitude since they're comparing actual task scores. Let me verify what score comparison actually measures—it should be cases where neither dies or both die, and we're comparing their performance scores directly. I'm breaking down the episode counts to verify the penalty-independence calculation: death asymmetry cases (where only one agent dies) total 75 episodes across both preference directions, and these would maintain their labels regardless of penalty changes since the outcome is determined by survival alone. The remaining episodes either involve both agents dying or neither dying, which means their labels depend on accumulated scores rather than just death outcomes.

For the both-die scenarios, even though the penalty changes, it affects both agents equally, so the score difference—which determines the label—stays constant. The neither-die episodes have no death involvement whatsoever, making them purely task-performance based. This means all 95 labeled episodes are actually independent of penalty changes.

The 78.9% metric captures what fraction of the routing signal comes specifically from asymmetric death outcomes rather than score comparisons. It's penalty-independent by design—whether death costs -10 or -1000, exactly 75 of the 95 labels are determined by one agent dying while the other survives. This makes it a useful companion to DAF, measuring something slightly different but complementary: the proportion of non-tie labels explained by death asymmetry rather than task performance.

Now I'm looking at the Pearson correlation robustness across the 11 tasks in plan-012-full, which shows r=0.9698 with strong significance. To test this with Spearman ρ and leave-one-out validation, I need to extract per-task death rate differences and Δ_full values from the episodes.jsonl files, then compute both correlation coefficients with bootstrap confidence intervals.

The find-non-living-thing task stands out as the key leverage point—it has twice the sample size of other tasks, zero death rate in both modes, and all its +9.58 delta comes from non-death performance differences, making it structurally different from the death-dominated signal in the rest of the data. Extracting and aggregating this data from the results directory should take about 30 minutes on CPU.

For the majority-class baseline, the label distribution shows 95 non-tie labels out of 245 total, with det_preferred dominating at 86 cases.  However, the router's 65.4% accuracy is evaluated against the validation set (306 steps), where det_preferred appears 273 times, giving a baseline of 89.2%—this is the relevant comparison point for assessing the router's performance.

The distinction between the 90.5% episode-level baseline and 89.2% step-level baseline matters less for the qualitative claim that the router underperforms, but for accuracy I should use the step-level baseline since that's what the router is actually evaluated against. The real takeaway is that the router's balanced accuracy of around 58% is only marginally better than random guessing at 50%, which supports the point that this diagnostic probe is quite weak. The 58.1% figure comes from averaging the balanced accuracy across four seeds (59.27%, 57.36%, 57.48%, 58.31%), while 59.3% is just the single seed 42 result rounded up. For the paper, I should report the 4-seed mean of 58.1% with its standard deviation rather than cherry-picking the highest individual seed value.

On the Δ_nd notation question, introducing a prime symbol (Δ'_nd) just for one appendix number creates unnecessary confusion. It's cleaner to keep Δ_nd=2.84 consistent throughout and simply explain in the appendix that the penalty-sensitivity analysis uses a different calculation method—applying the penalty replacement across all 245 episodes rather than just the 135 neither-die episodes from the main text.

For the contribution framing, since empirical findings don't require methodological novelty, positioning this as an empirical discovery paired with a reproducible analysis framework is the right approach. Now I'll draft the review.

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Tool-Use Learning.}
4	Toolformer \cite{toolformer-2024} inserts API calls via self-supervised filtering; ToolkenGPT \cite{toolkengpt-2023} represents tools as embeddings in the token space; ART \cite{art-2023} and Chameleon \cite{chameleon-2024} compose tools under LLM orchestration; ReTool \cite{retool-2026} applies RL for code-interpreter invocation.
5	We differ by generating routing labels via \emph{episode-level counterfactual analysis}, framing routing as \emph{catastrophic failure prevention}, and training an explicit lightweight router for diagnostic analysis.
6	
7	\paragraph{Adaptive Computation.}
8	CogRouter \cite{cogrouter-2026} routes among cognitive depth levels on ScienceWorld; ARES \cite{ares-2026} selects per-step reasoning effort; Mixture-of-Recursions \cite{mixture-of-recursions-2025} applies adaptive depth at the architecture level.
9	MTRouter \cite{mtrouter-2026} routes among LLMs to optimize cost-quality tradeoffs.
10	These methods route \emph{how much} or \emph{which model}; we route \emph{which modality} and find that death avoidance accounts for ${\sim}90\%$ of the routing signal.
11	
12	\paragraph{World Model Routing.}
13	ITP \cite{itp-2026} and Dyna-Think \cite{dynathink-2025} route to approximate LLM-based world models that inherit hallucination failures.
14	Our deterministic modality provides exact state transitions, and our analysis reveals that this exactness matters almost exclusively for death avoidance.
15	
16	\paragraph{Agent Frameworks.}
17	ReAct \cite{react-2023} interleaves reasoning and acting and serves as our internal-reasoning baseline. ALFWorld \cite{alfworld-2021} provides a text-based household benchmark complementary to ScienceWorld; Voyager \cite{voyager-2023} demonstrates open-ended LLM agents with learned skill libraries.
18	We analyze \emph{when} reasoning should yield to deterministic computation, finding the answer concentrated in catastrophic failure scenarios. Our finding connects to the safe RL literature, where avoiding catastrophic states is formalized as a hard constraint \citep{garcia2015comprehensive}.
19	
20	\paragraph{Concurrent Work.}
21	Concurrent studies examine complementary aspects: cognitive illusions driving tool overuse \cite{tool-overuse-illusion-2026}, hidden-state encoding of tool-call decisions \cite{when2tool-2026}, a ``tool-use tax'' where tools are not always beneficial \cite{tool-use-tax-2026}, and necessity/utility/affordability evaluation \cite{to-call-or-not-2026}. Our death avoidance decomposition provides concrete quantification---${\sim}90\%$ of routing signal stems from catastrophic-failure prevention---grounding these frameworks in interactive-environment evidence.
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	
3	\subsection{Death Avoidance Decomposition}
4	\label{sec:death-decomp}
5	
6	\begin{figure}[t]
7	\centering
8	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_daf_scatter.pdf}
9	\caption{Per-task scatter plot: death rate differential (internal minus deterministic) vs.\ performance gap ($\Delta_{\text{full}}$). Pearson $r = 0.9698$ ($p = 8.12 \times 10^{-7}$). Each point is one of 11 ScienceWorld task types.}
10	\label{fig:decomposition}
11	\end{figure}
12	
13	The full performance gap between deterministic and internal strategies across 245 episodes is $\Delta_{\text{full}} = +29.60$ points. When we exclude all episodes where either strategy results in agent death, the gap collapses to $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$. The resulting Death Avoidance Factor (\S\ref{sec:daf}) is 90.41\%: approximately 90\% of the deterministic strategy's advantage over internal reasoning is attributable to death avoidance rather than superior task performance when both agents survive.
14	
15	This concentration is consistent across task types (Appendix~\ref{app:per-task}). The Pearson correlation between per-task death rate differential and $\Delta_{\text{full}}$ is $r = 0.9698$ ($p = 8.12 \times 10^{-7}$; bootstrap 95\% CI $[0.952, 0.995]$), as shown in Figure~\ref{fig:decomposition}. Spearman rank correlation is $\rho = 0.986$ ($p = 2.4 \times 10^{-8}$), and leave-one-out analysis shows $r \in [0.963, 0.980]$---no single task type is a leverage point (Appendix~\ref{app:correlation}). Knowing how much more frequently the internal strategy dies is nearly sufficient to predict the full performance gap for a given task type. The internal strategy dies in 42.9\% of episodes compared to 16.3\% for deterministic computation---a 2.6$\times$ ratio that drives the bulk of the observed score difference.
16	
17	The episode-level label distribution reflects this asymmetry: of 245 episodes, 86 are \texttt{det\_preferred}, only 9 \texttt{internal\_preferred}, and 150 \texttt{no\_preference}---when internal reasoning does not cause death, it performs comparably to deterministic computation.
18	
19	One task reveals the residual non-death signal. \texttt{find-non-living-thing} ($n=40$) has zero deaths under both strategies yet shows $\Delta_{\text{full}} = +9.58$, entirely from non-death performance differences---deterministic computation can provide advantages through more accurate state tracking even when agents survive. However, among the 10 death-prone task types, death avoidance overwhelms non-death differences. When both modalities survive, the gap is small but non-negligible ($\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$, 9.6\% of $\Delta_{\text{full}}$).
20	
21	\subsection{Baselines}
22	\label{sec:baselines}
23	
24	\begin{table}[t]
25	\centering
26	\small
27	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
28	\toprule
29	Strategy & Score & 95\% CI & Death \% \\
30	\midrule
31	Always-Internal & $-39.07$ & [$-46.40$, $-32.27$] & 42.9 \\
32	Always-Deterministic & $-9.47$ & [$-14.71$, $-4.29$] & 16.3 \\
33	Random & $-22.38$ & [$-28.67$, $-15.75$] & 27.8 \\
34	Oracle & $-6.58$ & [$-11.54$, $-1.70$] & 14.3 \\
35	\midrule
36	Trained Router & $-10.70$ & [$-16.33$, $-5.27$] & 17.6 \\
37	\bottomrule
38	\end{tabular}
39	\caption{Episode-weighted mean scores on ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes). 95\% Bootstrap CIs. The oracle selects the higher-scoring strategy per episode; 96.3\% of its decisions match the always-deterministic strategy.}
40	\label{tab:baselines}
41	\end{table}
42	
43	Table~\ref{tab:baselines} reports episode-weighted mean scores and death rates for four unconditional strategies. Always-deterministic ($-9.47$) outperforms always-internal ($-39.07$) by 29.60 points, consistent with $\Delta_{\text{full}}$. The oracle, which selects the higher-scoring strategy per episode, improves only modestly to $-6.58$, with 96.3\% of its routing decisions matching always-deterministic. This \emph{oracle degeneracy} indicates that the optimal episode-level routing policy closely resembles unconditional deterministic computation---the routing signal is insufficient to improve upon simply always choosing the deterministic strategy. Random routing ($-22.38$) falls between the two pure strategies, with a death rate (27.8\%) proportional to its 50/50 allocation.
44	
45	Keyword-based routing fails structurally: match rates cluster at extremes (85.8\% or $<$5\%), precluding meaningful routing discrimination.
46	
47	\subsection{Router Training as Diagnostic}
48	\label{sec:router-diagnostic}
49	
50	A natural question is whether the episode-level counterfactual labels can train a step-level router. We investigate this as a diagnostic probe of label informativeness rather than a routing system proposal, testing two architectures.
51	
52	\paragraph{Classification head.} We append a linear classification head to the LLM's hidden representations, trained on episode-level routing labels. Across all configurations tested---class weighting, focal loss, minority undersampling, and temperature scaling---the classifier degenerates to predicting always-deterministic (0\% internal recall in all cases). The episode-level labeling granularity does not transfer to step-level classification through this architecture.
53	
54	\paragraph{Implicit router.} We fine-tune Qwen3-1.7B to internalize routing decisions within its generation process (training details in Appendix~\ref{app:router-details}). At both the logit and generation levels, accuracy is 65.4\%, balanced accuracy $58.1 \pm 0.8\%$ (4-seed mean$\pm$std), and internal recall 51.5\%---above chance but insufficient for practical routing. With only 81 training episodes (14 validation), we cannot conclusively distinguish structurally thin routing signal from data insufficiency. Cross-seed balanced accuracy is stable (57--59\%), though internal recall varies widely (51.5--81.8\%; Appendix~\ref{app:router-details}).
55	
56	\paragraph{End-to-end evaluation.} Deployed as an episode-level router (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}), the trained router scores $-10.70$ (95\% CI $[-16.33, -5.27]$), not statistically outperforming always-deterministic ($-9.47$, $[-14.71, -4.29]$; overlapping 95\% CIs). The router's $58.1\%$ balanced accuracy falls below the step-level majority-class baseline of 89.2\% (273/306 deterministic-preferred validation steps). Logit-level success rules out uninformative labels, pointing to the episode-to-step granularity mismatch as the bottleneck. When DAF $\approx 90\%$, router headroom is structurally limited to the ${\sim}10\%$ non-death signal, and any router that occasionally routes internally risks catastrophic failure.
57	
58	\paragraph{Data split.} Of the 245 collected episodes, 95 receive a non-tie label (det-preferred or internal-preferred) and are used for router training. We apply episode-stratified 85/15 train/validation split (81 train, 14 validation episodes; 1734 train, 306 validation steps). Table~\ref{tab:baselines} baselines (always-internal, always-deterministic, random) report descriptive statistics of collected trajectory scores, while the trained router score ($-10.70$) comes from fresh environment interaction (end-to-end evaluation on new episodes), not evaluation on training data.
59	
60	\paragraph{Labeling quality.} We verify label quality in Appendix~\ref{app:labeling}.
```

When we exclude all episodes where either strategy results in agent death, the gap collapses to $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$. The resulting Death Avoidance Factor (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}) is 90.41\\%: approximately 90\\% of the deterministic strategy's advantage over internal reasoning is attributable to death avoidance rather than superior task performance when both agents survive.\n\nThis concentration is consistent across task types (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-task}). Knowing how much more frequently the internal strategy dies is nearly sufficient to predict the full performance gap for a given task type. <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018AGcZUNk5Woa1LBpKfn4VU`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	When a Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct agent attempts to boil water in ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}, it must track temperature thresholds and container states across dozens of steps. Internal reasoning hallucinates these transitions---predicting water has boiled when the stove was never activated---triggering agent death with a $-100$ penalty. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates such hallucinations via exact state transitions. The natural question: when should an agent route computation externally versus reasoning internally?
4	
5	Existing routing approaches \cite{toolformer-2024,art-2023,chameleon-2024} frame tool selection as a capability or efficiency decision---matching subtasks to tools that can solve them. This framing ignores a potentially dominant signal: routing to \emph{avoid catastrophic failure}. Understanding what routing actually learns requires counterfactual analysis, but the standard per-step oracle approach degenerates under sparse rewards. On ScienceWorld, where non-zero reward arrives only at episode termination, 97.4\% of steps (across 11 prescreened task types) produce identical scores regardless of modality---no step-level signal exists to train a router (\S\ref{sec:collection}). Keyword-based heuristics (\S\ref{sec:baselines}) also fail structurally: ScienceWorld actions use generic verbs (\texttt{pour}, \texttt{move}, \texttt{activate}) rather than science-specific terminology, providing no lexical basis for routing decisions.
6	
7	We propose \emph{episode-level counterfactual analysis}: run complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct~\cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, then compare episode outcomes to generate routing labels (\S\ref{sec:collection}). Across 11 ScienceWorld task types (245 episodes), the full deterministic advantage is $\Delta_{\text{full}} = +29.60$ points. Excluding episodes where either strategy causes agent death, this gap collapses to $\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$---a Death Avoidance Factor (DAF; \S\ref{sec:daf}) of 90.41\%. The per-task correlation between death rate differential and performance gap is $r = 0.9698$ ($p = 8.12 \times 10^{-7}$), confirming that death avoidance is the primary mechanism across task types (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}). Death rates differ sharply (42.9\% internal vs.\ 16.3\% deterministic), and 96.3\% of oracle routing decisions match always-deterministic (\S\ref{sec:baselines}).
8	
9	Our contributions: (1) \emph{identifying step-level oracle degeneracy} in sparse-reward environments and applying episode-level counterfactual analysis to diagnose routing signal composition via the DAF metric (\S\ref{sec:collection}--\ref{sec:daf}); and (2) the empirical finding that \emph{death avoidance accounts for 90.41\% of routing signal} on ScienceWorld, with a trained router failing to outperform always-deterministic (\S\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).
```

My role is read-only: audit experiments, verify conclusions, check GPU compliance, and post findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. All output must be in Chinese. Throughout this session I have been responding to system-triggered review requests as the paper progresses through multiple review iterations (R1→R5), verifying data consistency, checking Action Matrices, and flagging issues.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Death Avoidance Factor (DAF)**: Core paper finding — ~90% of routing signal between internal LLM reasoning and deterministic computation on ScienceWorld is attributable to death avoidance (avoiding -100 penalty)
   - **Episode-level counterfactual analysis**: The methodology — run complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies, compare outcomes
   - **Key metrics**: Δ_full=+29.60, Δ_nd=+2.84, DAF=90.41%, Pearson r=0.9698 (p=8.12e-07), 11 task types, 245 episodes
   - **Penalty sensitivity**: DAF ranges from 46.4% (P=10) to 94.5% (P=200), monotonically increasing
   - **Death-asymmetry ratio**: 75/95=78.9% of non-tie labels determined by asymmetric death events (penalty-independent companion metric)
   - **Step-level tie rate**: 97.4% (6094 steps, 5935 ties) — proves per-step oracle labeling degenerates
   - **Router training**: Qwen3-1.7B implicit router, balanced_acc=58.1±0.8% (4-seed), episode-stratified 85/15 train/val split via sklearn
   - **Label distribution**: 86 det-preferred, 9 internal-preferred, 150 no-preference (out of 245 episodes)
   - **Registry entries**: plan-012-full (canonical death decomposition), plan-012-penalty-sensitivity (post-hoc DAF at various penalties), plan-013-labeling-quality (label quality analysis), plan-004-ablation-b (router training)
   - **Server**: westb-13313, 2× RTX 5090 32GB (cuda:0, cuda:1)

        "content": "\\section{Experiments}\n\n\\subsection{Death Avoidance Decomposition}\n\\label{sec:death-decomp}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig1_daf_scatter.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-task scatter plot: death rate differential (internal minus deterministic) vs.\\ performance gap ($\\Delta_{\\text{full}}$). Pearson $r = 0.9698$ ($p = 8.12 \\times 10^{-7}$). Each point is one of 11 ScienceWorld task types.}\n\\label{fig:decomposition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe full performance gap between deterministic and internal strategies across 245 episodes is $\\Delta_{\\text{full}} = +29.60$ points. When we exclude all episodes where either strategy results in agent death, the gap collapses to $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$. The resulting Death Avoidance Factor (\\S\\ref{sec:daf}) is 90.41\\%: approximately 90\\% of the deterministic strategy's advantage over internal reasoning is attributable to death avoidance rather than superior task performance when both agents survive.\n\nThis concentration is consistent across task types (Appendix~\\ref{app:per-task}). The Pearson correlation between per-task death rate differential and $\\Delta_{\\text{full}}$ is $r = 0.9698$ ($p = 8.12 \\times 10^{-7}$; bootstrap 95\\% CI $[0.952, 0.995]$), as shown in Figure~\\ref{fig:decomposition}. Spearman rank correlation is $\\rho = 0.986$ ($p = 2.4 \\times 10^{-8}$), and leave-one-out analysis shows $r \\in [0.963, 0.980]$---no single task type is a leverage point (Appendix~\\ref{app:correlation}). Knowing how much more frequently the internal strategy dies is nearly sufficient to predict the full performance gap for a given task type. The internal strategy dies in 42.9\\% of episodes compared to 16.3\\% for deterministic computation---a 2.6$\\times$ ratio that drives the bulk of the observed score difference.\n\nThe episode-level label distribution reflects this asymmetry: of 245 episodes, 86 are \\texttt{det\\_preferred}, only 9 \\texttt{internal\\_preferred}, and 150 \\texttt{no\\_preference}---when internal reasoning does not cause death, it performs comparably to deterministic computation.\n\nOne task reveals the residual non-death signal. \\texttt{find-non-living-thing} ($n=40$) has zero deaths under both strategies yet shows $\\Delta_{\\text{full}} = +9.58$, entirely from non-death performance differences---deterministic computation can provide advantages through more accurate state tracking even when agents survive. However, among the 10 death-prone task types, death avoidance overwhelms non-death differences. When both modalities survive, the gap is small but non-negligible ($\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$, 9.6\\% of $\\Delta_{\\text{full}}$).\n\n\\subsection{Baselines}\n\\label{sec:baselines}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nStrategy & Score & 95\\% CI & Death \\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nAlways-Internal & $-39.07$ & [$-46.40$, $-32.27$] & 42.9 \\\\\nAlways-Deterministic & $-9.47$ & [$-14.71$, $-4.29$] & 16.3 \\\\\nRandom & $-22.38$ & [$-28.67$, $-15.75$] & 27.8 \\\\\nOracle & $-6.58$ & [$-11.54$, $-1.70$] & 14.3 \\\\\n\\midrule\nTrained Router & $-10.70$ & [$-16.33$, $-5.27$] & 17.6 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Episode-weighted mean scores on ScienceWorld (11 task types, 245 episodes). 95\\% Bootstrap CIs. The oracle selects the higher-scoring strategy per episode; 96.3\\% of its decisions match the always-deterministic strategy.}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:baselines} reports episode-weighted mean scores and death rates for four unconditional strategies. Always-deterministic ($-9.47$) outperforms always-internal ($-39.07$) by 29.60 points, consistent with $\\Delta_{\\text{full}}$. The oracle, which selects the higher-scoring strategy per episode, improves only modestly to $-6.58$, with 96.3\\% of its routing decisions matching always-deterministic. This \\emph{oracle degeneracy} indicates that the optimal episode-level routing policy closely resembles unconditional deterministic computation---the routing signal is insufficient to improve upon simply always choosing the deterministic strategy. Random routing ($-22.38$) falls between the two pure strategies, with a death rate (27.8\\%) proportional to its 50/50 allocation.\n\nKeyword-based routing fails structurally: match rates cluster at extremes (85.8\\% or $<$5\\%), precluding meaningful routing discrimination.\n\n\\subsection{Router Training as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:router-diagnostic}\n\nA natural question is whether the episode-level counterfactual labels can train a step-level router. We investigate this as a diagnostic probe of label informativeness rather than a routing system proposal, testing two architectures.\n\n\\paragraph{Classification head.} We append a linear classification head to the LLM's hidden representations, trained on episode-level routing labels. Across all configurations tested---class weighting, focal loss, minority undersampling, and temperature scaling---the classifier degenerates to predicting always-deterministic (0\\% internal recall in all cases). The episode-level labeling granularity does not transfer to step-level classification through this architecture.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit router.} We fine-tune Qwen3-1.7B to internalize routing decisions within its generation process (training details in Appendix~\\ref{app:router-details}). At both the logit and generation levels, accuracy is 65.4\\%, balanced accuracy $58.1 \\pm 0.8\\%$ (4-seed mean$\\pm$std), and internal recall 51.5\\%---above chance but insufficient for practical routing. With only 81 training episodes (14 validation), we cannot conclusively distinguish structurally thin routing signal from data insufficiency. Cross-seed balanced accuracy is stable (57--59\\%), though internal recall varies widely (51.5--81.8\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:router-details}).\n\n\\paragraph{End-to-end evaluation.} Deployed as an episode-level router (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}), the trained router scores $-10.70$ (95\\% CI $[-16.33, -5.27]$), not statistically outperforming always-deterministic ($-9.47$, $[-14.71, -4.29]$; overlapping 95\\% CIs). The router's $58.1\\%$ balanced accuracy falls below the step-level majority-class baseline of 89.2\\% (273/306 deterministic-preferred validation steps). Logit-level success rules out uninformative labels, pointing to the episode-to-step granularity mismatch as the bottleneck. When DAF $\\approx 90\\%$, router headroom is structurally limited to the ${\\sim}10\\%$ non-death signal, and any router that occasionally routes internally risks catastrophic failure.\n\n\\paragraph{Data split.} Of the 245 collected episodes, 95 receive a non-tie label (det-preferred or internal-preferred) and are used for router training. We apply episode-stratified 85/15 train/validation split (81 train, 14 validation episodes; 1734 train, 306 validation steps). Table~\\ref{tab:baselines} baselines (always-internal, always-deterministic, random) report descriptive statistics of collected trajectory scores, while the trained router score ($-10.70$) comes from fresh environment interaction (end-to-end evaluation on new episodes), not evaluation on training data.\n\n\\paragraph{Labeling quality.} We verify label quality in Appendix~\\ref{app:labeling}.",

        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nWhen a Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct agent attempts to boil water in ScienceWorld \\cite{scienceworld-2022}, it must track temperature thresholds and container states across dozens of steps. Internal reasoning hallucinates these transitions---predicting water has boiled when the stove was never activated---triggering agent death with a $-100$ penalty. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates such hallucinations via exact state transitions. The natural question: when should an agent route computation externally versus reasoning internally?\n\nExisting routing approaches \\cite{toolformer-2024,art-2023,chameleon-2024} frame tool selection as a capability or efficiency decision---matching subtasks to tools that can solve them. This framing ignores a potentially dominant signal: routing to \\emph{avoid catastrophic failure}. Understanding what routing actually learns requires counterfactual analysis, but the standard per-step oracle approach degenerates under sparse rewards. On ScienceWorld, where non-zero reward arrives only at episode termination, 97.4\\% of steps (across 11 prescreened task types) produce identical scores regardless of modality---no step-level signal exists to train a router (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}). Keyword-based heuristics (\\S\\ref{sec:baselines}) also fail structurally: ScienceWorld actions use generic verbs (\\texttt{pour}, \\texttt{move}, \\texttt{activate}) rather than science-specific terminology, providing no lexical basis for routing decisions.\n\nWe propose \\emph{episode-level counterfactual analysis}: run complete episodes under pure-internal (ReAct~\\cite{react-2023}) and pure-deterministic strategies, then compare episode outcomes to generate routing labels (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}). Across 11 ScienceWorld task types (245 episodes), the full deterministic advantage is $\\Delta_{\\text{full}} = +29.60$ points. Excluding episodes where either strategy causes agent death, this gap collapses to $\\Delta_{\\text{nd}} = +2.84$---a Death Avoidance Factor (DAF; \\S\\ref{sec:daf}) of 90.41\\%. The per-task correlation between death rate differential and performance gap is $r = 0.9698$ ($p = 8.12 \\times 10^{-7}$), confirming that death avoidance is the primary mechanism across task types (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}). Death rates differ sharply (42.9\\% internal vs.\\ 16.3\\% deterministic), and 96.3\\% of oracle routing decisions match always-deterministic (\\S\\ref{sec:baselines}).\n\nOur contributions: (1) \\emph{identifying step-level oracle degeneracy} in sparse-reward environments and applying episode-level counterfactual analysis to diagnose routing signal composition via the DAF metric (\\S\\ref{sec:collection}--\\ref{sec:daf}); and (2) the empirical finding that \\emph{death avoidance accounts for 90.41\\% of routing signal} on ScienceWorld, with a trained router failing to outperform always-deterministic (\\S\\ref{sec:death-decomp}--\\ref{sec:router-diagnostic}).",
        "numLines": 9,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 9
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/introduction.tex"
  },

    "content": {

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Tool-Use Learning.}\nToolformer \\cite{toolformer-2024} inserts API calls via self-supervised filtering; ToolkenGPT \\cite{toolkengpt-2023} represents tools as embeddings in the token space; ART \\cite{art-2023} and Chameleon \\cite{chameleon-2024} compose tools under LLM orchestration; ReTool \\cite{retool-2026} applies RL for code-interpreter invocation.\nWe differ by generating routing labels via \\emph{episode-level counterfactual analysis}, framing routing as \\emph{catastrophic failure prevention}, and training an explicit lightweight router for diagnostic analysis.\n\n\\paragraph{Adaptive Computation.}\nCogRouter \\cite{cogrouter-2026} routes among cognitive depth levels on ScienceWorld; ARES \\cite{ares-2026} selects per-step reasoning effort; Mixture-of-Recursions \\cite{mixture-of-recursions-2025} applies adaptive depth at the architecture level.\nMTRouter \\cite{mtrouter-2026} routes among LLMs to optimize cost-quality tradeoffs.\nThese methods route \\emph{how much} or \\emph{which model}; we route \\emph{which modality} and find that death avoidance accounts for ${\\sim}90\\%$ of the routing signal.\n\n\\paragraph{World Model Routing.}\nITP \\cite{itp-2026} and Dyna-Think \\cite{dynathink-2025} route to approximate LLM-based world models that inherit hallucination failures.\nOur deterministic modality provides exact state transitions, and our analysis reveals that this exactness matters almost exclusively for death avoidance.\n\n\\paragraph{Agent Frameworks.}\nReAct \\cite{react-2023} interleaves reasoning and acting and serves as our internal-reasoning baseline. ALFWorld \\cite{alfworld-2021} provides a text-based household benchmark complementary to ScienceWorld; Voyager \\cite{voyager-2023} demonstrates open-ended LLM agents with learned skill libraries.\nWe analyze \\emph{when} reasoning should yield to deterministic computation, finding the answer concentrated in catastrophic failure scenarios. Our finding connects to the safe RL literature, where avoiding catastrophic states is formalized as a hard constraint \\citep{garcia2015comprehensive}.\n\n\\paragraph{Concurrent Work.}\nConcurrent studies examine complementary aspects: cognitive illusions driving tool overuse \\cite{tool-overuse-illusion-2026}, hidden-state encoding of tool-call decisions \\cite{when2tool-2026}, a ``tool-use tax'' where tools are not always beneficial \\cite{tool-use-tax-2026}, and necessity/utility/affordability evaluation \\cite{to-call-or-not-2026}. Our death avoidance decomposition provides concrete quantification---${\\sim}90\\%$ of routing signal stems from catastrophic-failure prevention---grounding these frameworks in interactive-environment evidence.",
        "numLines": 21,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 21
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/related_work.tex"
  },

The paper only reports the single seed-42 result of 51.5%, so this broader range suggests the metric is quite sensitive to the random seed choice.

Moving to accuracy, I'm calculating the mean across the four seeds at around 51.4% and working through the variance calculation to get the standard deviation.

Now I'm seeing the real problem with how CR4 is being reported. The paper is inconsistently cherry-picking results—using the best-performing seed for accuracy at 65.4%, the correctly-aggregated four-seed mean for balanced accuracy, but then using the worst-performing seed for internal recall at 51.5%. This creates a misleading picture where accuracy looks artificially high while internal recall appears artificially low.

The Director is asking whether to label these as "(best seed)" or standardize everything to four-seed means. I think the answer is clear: consistency matters more than highlighting individual seeds, especially since seed 42 isn't actually best across all metrics. If I switch to four-seed reporting across the board, the accuracy drops to 51.4% (which is actually below the majority-class baseline of 89.2%), and internal recall rises to 66.7%—both of which actually strengthen the paper's core argument that the router can't reliably beat a deterministic approach.

The high variance in these numbers (±12% for accuracy, ±14.4% for internal recall) further supports the instability narrative. And since the paper's main comparison is already using balanced accuracy at 58.1%, which is already four-seed reported, I should keep that consistent and move everything else to the same standard.

Looking back at what the paper already states, it acknowledges the cross-seed variance in internal recall (51.5--81.8%) in the appendix reference, so the real problem is mixing seed 42 results with 4-seed means in the headline numbers. The cleanest solution is to pick one approach: either stick with 4-seed means throughout for transparency, or lead with balanced accuracy and explicitly note where the other metrics vary across seeds. <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012Auv3E3uwnjceZekgBr95g`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We presented episode-level counterfactual analysis for diagnosing routing behavior in modular LLM agents. Applied to 245 episodes across 11 ScienceWorld task types, death avoidance accounts for 90.41\% of the routing signal under the default $-100$ penalty ($\Delta_{\text{full}}$ = +29.60 vs.\ $\Delta_{\text{nd}}$ = +2.84; 78.9\% of non-tie labels from death asymmetry), corroborated by near-perfect cross-task correlation ($r$ = 0.9698; Spearman $\rho$ = 0.986), oracle degeneracy (96.3\% agreement with always-deterministic), and near-random trained-router accuracy ($58.1 \pm 0.8\%$). A preliminary MATH pilot (50 problems) and an exploratory ALFWorld study (135 games) provide cross-environment evidence: MATH shows vanishing routing signal absent catastrophic failure, while ALFWorld exhibits the same deterministic-preferred asymmetry (25.9\% vs.\ 1.5\%)---consistent with catastrophic failure prevention being a cross-environment phenomenon, though the specific mechanism varies (death avoidance in ScienceWorld vs.\ action space pruning in ALFWorld).
```

</details>

```text
25	\input{related_work}
26	\input{conclusion}
27	
28	\section*{Limitations}
29	
30	Our analysis uses a single benchmark (ScienceWorld) with a single LLM backbone (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct), and generalization to other interactive environments and model scales remains to be established. The $-100$ death penalty may amplify DAF; environments with less catastrophic penalties could exhibit lower DAF, though the qualitative finding may still hold. On the 135 episodes (55.1\%) where neither strategy leads to catastrophic failure, deterministic computation retains a small advantage ($\Delta_{\text{nd}} = +2.84$), indicating non-zero routing signal beyond death avoidance. Our analysis covers 11 of 30 ScienceWorld task types; the remaining 19 (63\%) were excluded during prescreening because both strategies timed out on all seed variations within the 30-step budget, providing no counterfactual signal. Stronger models (e.g., 70B-scale or reasoning-specialized) likely exhibit lower internal-strategy death rates, which would reduce DAF. Our finding may reflect a capability-dependent failure mode: at higher capability levels, death avoidance may no longer dominate routing signal.
31	
32	\paragraph{Cross-environment evidence.}
33	A preliminary 50-problem MATH Level~4--5 pilot, using code interpreter as the deterministic modality, provides initial evidence beyond ScienceWorld. Internal reasoning achieved 80\% accuracy versus 72\% for code interpreter, with 88\% of problems showing no modality preference (1 deterministic-preferred, 5 internal-preferred, 44 no-preference). In the absence of death risk, the routing signal vanishes or reverses---consistent with death avoidance as the primary driver. An exploratory ALFWorld study \cite{alfworld-2021} (135 games, 6 task types) shows a related but mechanistically distinct pattern: 25.9\% of episodes are deterministic-preferred versus 1.5\% internal-preferred, with deterministic success rate (27.4\%) far exceeding internal (3.0\%). However, the underlying mechanism differs from ScienceWorld. In ScienceWorld, the internal strategy has baseline competence (57.1\% survival rate) but suffers hallucination-induced catastrophic failures; in ALFWorld, the 3\% internal success rate reflects a fundamental capability gap---without admissible action filtering, the agent rarely completes tasks at all. The deterministic advantage in ALFWorld thus stems from action space pruning rather than death avoidance per se. These results are suggestive but do not directly confirm that the DAF pattern generalizes; environments with intermediate failure modes remain to be studied.
34	
35	\paragraph{Step-level mislabeling.} Episode-level labels may assign suboptimal routing decisions to individual steps; in particular, steps preceding a death event are labeled det-preferred even if the death was not foreseeable from those steps' observations. Training on pure-strategy trajectories but deploying on mixed policy also introduces a distribution shift \citep{dagger-2011}.
36	
37	\paragraph{Scope.}
38	Extending the counterfactual methodology to environments with graded failure costs, embodied agents, or multi-turn dialogue systems would clarify how broadly death avoidance accounts for apparent routing signal. Step-level labeling with denser rewards and the logit--generation gap in the implicit router are natural next steps. We audit labeling quality in Appendix~\ref{app:labeling}.
39	
40	\section*{Ethics Statement}
41	
42	This work uses ScienceWorld \cite{scienceworld-2022}, a publicly available text-based simulation environment, and does not involve human subjects or private data. All language models are accessed via standard APIs or publicly released weights. We report computational costs: approximately 40 GPU-hours on two RTX 5090 GPUs for data collection, router training, and evaluation.
43	
44	\bibliography{references}
45	
46	\appendix
47	
48	\section{Labeling Quality Audit}
49	\label{app:labeling}
50	
51	A potential concern is oracle circularity: whether episode-level labels reflect tie-breaking artifacts rather than genuine modality differences. We audit all 245 labels. Of the 150 \texttt{no\_preference} labels (61.2\%), 115 (46.9\%) are both-alive ties and 35 (14.3\%) are both-dead---all excluded from router training. The remaining 95 training labels are entirely genuine: 75 from death asymmetry (70 deterministic-preferred, 5 internal-preferred) and 20 from score comparison with both strategies surviving (16 deterministic-preferred, 4 internal-preferred). Zero labels depend on tie-breaking. Retraining the router using only death-asymmetry labels yields balanced accuracy of 57.3\%, within 1$\sigma$ of the full-label retrain (57.6 $\pm$ 0.5\%), confirming that score-comparison labels contribute negligible additional signal. The 97.4\% step-level tie rate (\S\ref{sec:collection}) further motivates episode-level over step-level labeling.
52	
53	\section{Per-Task Decomposition}
54	\label{app:per-task}
55	We select 11 of ScienceWorld's 30 task types via prescreening: task types where both strategies complete at least one episode within the 30-step budget are retained; types where both strategies time out on all seed variations are excluded.\footnote{Prescreening ensures that the counterfactual comparison is meaningful---task types where neither strategy makes progress provide no routing signal.}
56

[WRITE HANDOFF] 请立即写你的 handoff。

**格式硬约束**:
- 整条回复**只能**是 &lt;handoff>...&lt;/handoff> 标签包裹
- 禁止在标签外写任何寒暄 / 说明 / 确认（"好的"/"我开始写"/"写完了"等）

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff

示例格式:
&lt;handoff>
### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
- 本项目只能用 cuda:4 / cuda:5（之前两次违规 cuda:0 被 Director CRITICAL）
- submit_training_job 不支持 shell builtin（source/conda activate 全不行）

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
- 服务器：westd-8x6000，工作目录 /root/autodl-tmp/hybrid_reasoning_phases
- Python：/root/miniconda3/bin/python（绝对路径，不走 conda activate）
- 模型：/root/autodl-tmp/qwen35_4b_direct
- 活跃：pilot_011 (PID 203221, tmux=pilot_011, cuda:4)

### 3. 📊 当前状态
我刚完成 pilot_010 分析，正在等 pilot_011 (Falcon-H1R) CoT 生成完成（6/50）。
registry: pilot_011 RUNNING；其他全 DONE。

### 4. 📋 待办
- pilot_011 cron 回调 → spawn worker 跑 probe + GC4 计算
- GC4 ≥2pp → 转架构 narrative；<2pp → null-result paper outline 已就绪

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
Falcon-H1R 是最后一个数据点。已知 4B/9B GC4 都 FAIL。
